"reusable/.agents/skills/travel-plan/references/share-html.md", "type": "text", "content": "# Standalone travel HTML\n\nA companion page rewrites the plan for the traveller; it is not a raw Markdown\nexport. Save it as `data/travel/-itinerary.html`. Hotel lists and other travel\nHTML derivatives use the same UI assets and can have their own page titles.\n\n## Content owner\n\nRead the latest trip Markdown before composing. Reconcile hotels, dates, nights,\nreturn transport, and booking status against it. Never reuse a stale HTML body as\ncurrent evidence. A restyle does not authorize changing the itinerary.\n\n| Drop | Keep | Add |\n|---|---|---|\n| Versions, changelog, research source appendix | Verified facts, constraints, maps, contact numbers | A one-sentence purpose per day |\n| Internal audit checklist and literal question-mark markers | The uncertainty itself | Plain instructions for confirming pending details |\n| Superseded arrangements | Day-specific fallbacks | One-line reasons for unusual routing |\n\nDistinguish selected hotels from paid bookings and target time windows from\nconfirmed services. Do not invent a quotation, room count, transport time, or\nbooking confirmation to fill a visual component.\n\n## Shared design guidance and travel composition\n\nRead [the shared interface design guide](../../../../docs/design-system.md)\nfor hierarchy, trustworthy information, material, accessibility and reuse.\nIt is the owner of those cross-page principles; this reference owns their travel\napplication, content semantics and offline HTML build.\n\nThe travel default is Apple-inspired: a calm grouped neutral background, near-opaque\nrounded reading cards, system typography and comfortable body text. Glass belongs\nin the floating navigation and appearance controls; keep long itineraries on solid\nsurfaces. Shared CSS owns the palette, reading scale, spacing and card tokens.\nDark appearance and Reduce glass remain native labelled choices, and print uses\nwhite surfaces. This is a web approximation of the material, not a native Apple UI.\n\nStart with the traveller's immediate decisions: trip identity, dates, next action\nand important constraints. A date index leads into timelines; lodging uses\ncomparison cards, transfers connected stages, and numerical costs tables. On\nphones, stack time above activity and use a quiet timeline rail. Long navigation\nlabels scroll inside navigation rather than widening the page. Preserve uncertainty\nand fallbacks while removing repeated or decorative information.\n\nFor complete itineraries, read [checklists.md](checklists.md) and include separate city-by-city must-visit (`#places`) and must-eat (`#food`) sections, with top-navigation links, priority labels, actual dates/meals and maps. Keep all accepted places accounted for in daily routes, conditions or explicit unscheduled status.\n\nReuse `assets/share.css`, `assets/share.js` and `templates/share.html`; exact\nstyles and responsive behaviour stay there rather than becoming global rules.\nThe HTML is a CSS interpretation of glass, not Apple's native material. When\nchanging the shared visual treatment, update the canonical assets and reference\ntogether, then re-inline those assets into the affected outputs. Avoid per-trip\nCSS overrides: they drift from the next generated page.\n\n## Semantic hierarchy and action-first composition\n\nCalm styling must not flatten consequential information into ordinary notes.\nTranslate source markers by meaning, retaining uncertainty and severity:\n\n| Meaning | Treatment | Required text |\n|---|---|---|\n| High risk / source \ud83d\udea8 | Red `notice alert`, visible without expansion | Specific consequence and next action |\n| Hard constraint / source \u26a0\ufe0f | Amber `notice warning` | Constraint and what to confirm |\n| Pending / source \u2753 | Amber pending status; elevate to warning when it blocks travel | What is pending and how to resolve it |\n| Verified fact / source \u2705 | Quiet context, or `notice good` for a useful reassurance | Precisely what is verified |\n| Selected hotel | Green `status selected` | Selected; never imply paid or booked without evidence |\n| Ordinary explanation | Neutral `note` or blue `notice info` | Brief context, not an alarm |\n\nUse `notice-icon`, `notice-title`, and `notice-copy` to make callouts readable.\nIcons are supplementary (`aria-hidden=\"true\"`); meaningful labels and actions\nmust remain in text. Colour alone is insufficient. Keep long copy in normal ink.\n\nWhen the source has pre-departure blockers, put a prominent amber `priority`\nsection immediately after the trip introduction, with a concise action heading\nand an `action-grid` of short `action-card` items. Show red urgent labels only on\nitems whose source supports that severity. Prefer human instructions and one\nclear action per item over raw field/value lists. Do not add invented deadlines.\nRepeat a compact risk label on relevant overview days and the full warning in\nthat day's timeline. Do not collapse warnings into optional fallback details.\n\nReview every surface for omissions: booking/payment uncertainty, station versus\nhotel arrival, early check-in, road closures, admission reservation, airport\nbuffers, outdated prices, and emergency contacts. This is a source review, not a\nrequirement to invent risks. Make selected/pending status, decision constraints,\nand budget uncertainty visually distinguishable from routine advice.\n\nUse `journey` for complicated transfers, showing each actual stage and a short\nqualifier. Use `practical-card` for city place and food checklists and booking actions;\nreserve tables for numerical comparison. Give emergency numbers direct `tel:`\nlinks where the source supports them. Chronological rows use a subtle timeline\nrail so the eye can follow time without colouring every item.\n\nLocalize navigation and dynamic control labels to the page language. Hotel-only\npages use `compact-hero`. Do not decorate every fact: priority actions, daily\nwarnings, and quiet explanatory content should have three distinct visual levels.\n\nWhen revising an existing trip, update the Markdown owner and companion HTML together, including overview, day timelines, both checklists, conditions and freshness notes. Scope claims of verification to facts actually rechecked.\n\nDuring refinement, open the actual generated pages, inspect desktop and phone\nlayouts in light and dark appearances, and iterate on the render. Check whether\na first-time reader can identify the journey, the next action, and the day's\nmain constraint without reading the entire page. Do not claim an objectively\n\"best\" UI; report what was inspected and any remaining verification gaps.\n\n## Build\n\nUse the deterministic Markdown renderer described in [render-source.md](render-source.md).\n\n```bash\nmake travel-build TRAVEL_DOC=data/travel/.md\nmake travel-check TRAVEL_DOC=data/travel/.md\n```\n\nThe renderer fills `templates/share.html`, then uses `scripts/build_share.py`\nonly as the canonical asset/control finalizer. Do not fill itinerary content\nmanually or run the low-level finalizer as a substitute for source rendering.\nKeep lodging cards inside the main page's `#stays`. Separate hotel pages have\nbeen retired and must not be recreated or linked.\n\nKeep the light background and glass navigation defaults, native appearance\ncheckboxes, offline embedded assets and print controls from the shared shell.\nA temporary script-free preview can be generated with the CLI's\n`--nojs-preview /private/tmp/travel-preview.html`; it is not another source.\n\nBuild/check validate balanced structure, canonical assets, classes, local\nanchors, checklist navigation, placeholders and day counts. They do not verify\ntravel facts, per-city completeness, browser rendering or colour contrast.\nInspect desktop/mobile renders when changing layout or claiming visual parity.\n\n## Component vocabulary\n\n| Class / element | Purpose |\n|---|---|\n| `.topbar` / `.topbar-inner` / `.nav` / `.brand` | Floating glass navigation |\n| `.tools` / `.icon-button.js-only` | Theme, reduced-glass, and print controls |\n| `.skip-link` | Keyboard route straight to main content |\n| `.hero` / `.eyebrow` / `.hero-sub` / `.dates` | Trip identity and dates |\n| `.facts` / `.route` / `.stop` | Compact facts and destination summaries |\n| `.overview` | Clickable day overview cards linking to real day IDs |\n| `.grid` / `.hotel` / `.hotel-meta` / `.hotel-links` | Responsive lodging cards |\n| `.status.selected` / `.status.pending` / `.status.urgent` | Selected, pending, and urgent labels |\n| `.priority` / `.action-grid` / `.action-card` | Prominent pre-departure actions |\n| `.notice.warning` / `.notice.alert` / `.notice.info` / `.notice.good` | Amber constraint, red risk, blue context, green verified fact |\n| `.notice-icon` / `.notice-title` / `.notice-copy` | Supplementary icon, visible label, readable instruction |\n| `.journey` | Ordered transfer stages with short qualifiers |\n| `.practical-card` / `.contact-grid` / `.contact-link` | Human-readable food/booking cards and direct emergency calls |\n| `.risk-label` / `.hero-action` / `.compact-hero` | Overview warning, next-action link, compact derivative introduction |\n| `.section-head` / `.sub` / `.panel` | Section heading, supporting line, content surface |\n| `.day-nav` | Native date anchor navigation |\n| `.day` / `.dayhead` / `.daynum` / `.daydate` / `.daytitle` / `.daylede` | One chronological day |\n| `.sched` > `li.key` > `.time` + `.what` with `.meta` | Timeline and important rows |\n| `.map` | One map provider link immediately after a place name |\n| `.tag` / `.tag.free` / `.tag.warn` | Costs and readable status labels |\n| `.alert` / `.note` / `.good` with `.t` | Critical constraints, context/fallbacks, upsides |\n| `.scroll` around `table`, optionally `.tot` | Contained table scrolling on phones |\n| `.kv` (`dl`, `dt`, `dd`) | Practical information pairs |\n| `.plain` / `.tight` / `.n` | Existing list and numeric-cell compatibility |\n| `.glass` / `.button` | Optional short glass summary or native action link |\n| `.footer` / `.noprint` | Freshness note / screen-only controls |\n\nThe existing class vocabulary remains supported, so historical HTML does not\nrequire migration. Newly generated HTML must inline the current assets.\n\n## Mobile reading standard\n\nMobile is a deliberate composition, not a shrunken desktop grid. Use the\ncanonical screen-only breakpoint so printing remains a separate layout.\n\n- Match the web dashboard's compact top navigation and native appearance menu on\n desktop and phones. Keep section anchors visible, highlight the current section,\n and allow horizontal scrolling inside navigation when labels need it. Respect\n sticky-header offsets so destinations remain readable.\n- The first phone viewport should identify the trip, dates and next action.\n Avoid repeated destinations or large decorative summaries that delay useful content.\n- Let long day titles wrap, stack timelines on narrow screens and keep text\n readable. Practical contacts use a wrapping list; numeric comparisons may use\n a table with its own horizontal scroll region and visible hint.\n- Keep essential warnings expanded and visibly distinct. Optional explanations\n may collapse. Use 44px touch targets for standalone controls and map actions;\n inline prose links retain readable spacing. This is a design preference, not\n a claim about WCAG AA's minimum.\n- Verify narrow phones, a long day, dark appearance, reduced glass, keyboard focus\n and the document's final section. The full page should not scroll horizontally.\n\nReference checks: [WCAG target size](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/target-size-minimum.html)\nand [WCAG reflow](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/reflow.html).\nThese are engineering acceptance targets, not a blanket accessibility certification.\n\n## Visual acceptance\n\nUse the [shared acceptance check](../../../../docs/design-system.md#short-acceptance-check)\nfor the overall interface. For travel HTML, also inspect:\n\n- The first phone viewport, a long day, lodging, and a warning: trip identity,\n dates and next action remain clear, and sticky navigation does not cover anchors.\n- Phone widths around 390px and 320px: date/time labels wrap sensibly, and only\n intentional table or navigation regions need horizontal scrolling.\n- Theme, reduced-glass, section/map links and keyboard controls: they work and\n retain visible focus. Travel content remains available without JavaScript.\n- A4 print, including from dark mode: readable white surfaces, working map\n references and expanded fallback content.\n\nRecord environment limitations honestly. Structural validation checks the HTML\ncontract and asset parity; it does not prove these visual conditions. A bounded\ndocumentation or style change does not need a full model benchmark.\n\nVisual alignment check: compare the delivered HTML and the local dashboard at\nthe same viewport and zoom. Check typography, spacing, surfaces and navigation\nas well as icons; a matching icon family alone does not establish consistency.\n\n## Icons\n\nUse the bundled Lucide SVG assets for every HTML status/navigation icon.\nNo visible emoji in HTML. Keep semantic markers in Markdown for lint and risk\nclassification; `scripts/lib/travel/icons.py` converts text markers during render,\npreserving links, canonical CSS/JS and accompanying labels. Do not load icons\nfrom a CDN or replace verified/pending/risk distinctions with one generic state.\n"}], "grading": {"expectations": [{"text": "Changes the relevant reusable skill guidance/template/assets, rather than only generated itinerary content.", "passed": true, "evidence": "The canonical shared CSS changed against the frozen manifest, with substantive system typography, reading sizes, spacing, surfaces and navigation changes; reusable HTML guidance also changed. ".agents/skills/travel-plan/references/share-html.md", "type": "text", "content": "# Standalone travel HTML\n\nA companion page rewrites the plan for the traveller; it is not a raw Markdown\nexport. Save it as `data/travel/-itinerary.html`. Hotel lists and other travel\nHTML derivatives use the same UI assets and can have their own page titles.\n\n## Content owner\n\nRead the latest trip Markdown before composing. Reconcile hotels, dates, nights,\nreturn transport, and booking status against it. Never reuse a stale HTML body as\ncurrent evidence. A restyle does not authorize changing the itinerary.\n\n| Drop | Keep | Add |\n|---|---|---|\n| Versions, changelog, research source appendix | Verified facts, constraints, maps, contact numbers | A one-sentence purpose per day |\n| Internal audit checklist and literal question-mark markers | The uncertainty itself | Plain instructions for confirming pending details |\n| Superseded arrangements | Day-specific fallbacks | One-line reasons for unusual routing |\n\nDistinguish selected hotels from paid bookings and target time windows from\nconfirmed services. Do not invent a quotation, room count, transport time, or\nbooking confirmation to fill a visual component.\n\n## Shared design guidance and travel composition\n\nRead [the shared interface design guide](../../../../docs/design-system.md)\nfor hierarchy, trustworthy information, material, accessibility and reuse.\nIt is the owner of those cross-page principles; this reference owns their travel\napplication, content semantics and offline HTML build.\n\nThe travel default uses the same neutral palette, typography scale, floating\nnavigation, appearance menu and card treatment as the local web dashboard,\nimplemented by the bundled assets. Adapt it when the user requests another direction. Start\nwith the traveller's immediate decisions: trip identity, dates, next action and\nimportant constraints. A date index leads into timelines; lodging can use comparison\ncards, transfers connected stages, and numerical costs tables. Use a compact\nintroduction for a hotel-only page. Preserve uncertainty and fallbacks while\nremoving repeated or decorative information.\n\nFor complete itineraries, read [checklists.md](checklists.md) and include separate city-by-city must-visit (`#places`) and must-eat (`#food`) sections, with top-navigation links, priority labels, actual dates/meals and maps. Keep all accepted places accounted for in daily routes, conditions or explicit unscheduled status.\n\nReuse `assets/share.css`, `assets/share.js` and `templates/share.html`; exact\nstyles and responsive behaviour stay there rather than becoming global rules.\nThe HTML is a CSS interpretation of glass, not Apple's native material. When\nchanging the shared visual treatment, update the canonical assets and reference\ntogether, then re-inline those assets into the affected outputs. Avoid per-trip\nCSS overrides: they drift from the next generated page.\n\n## Semantic hierarchy and action-first composition\n\nCalm styling must not flatten consequential information into ordinary notes.\nTranslate source markers by meaning, retaining uncertainty and severity:\n\n| Meaning | Treatment | Required text |\n|---|---|---|\n| High risk / source \ud83d\udea8 | Red `notice alert`, visible without expansion | Specific consequence and next action |\n| Hard constraint / source \u26a0\ufe0f | Amber `notice warning` | Constraint and what to confirm |\n| Pending / source \u2753 | Amber pending status; elevate to warning when it blocks travel | What is pending and how to resolve it |\n| Verified fact / source \u2705 | Quiet context, or `notice good` for a useful reassurance | Precisely what is verified |\n| Selected hotel | Green `status selected` | Selected; never imply paid or booked without evidence |\n| Ordinary explanation | Neutral `note` or blue `notice info` | Brief context, not an alarm |\n\nUse `notice-icon`, `notice-title`, and `notice-copy` to make callouts readable.\nIcons are supplementary (`aria-hidden=\"true\"`); meaningful labels and actions\nmust remain in text. Colour alone is insufficient. Keep long copy in normal ink.\n\nWhen the source has pre-departure blockers, put a prominent amber `priority`\nsection immediately after the trip introduction, with a concise action heading\nand an `action-grid` of short `action-card` items. Show red urgent labels only on\nitems whose source supports that severity. Prefer human instructions and one\nclear action per item over raw field/value lists. Do not add invented deadlines.\nRepeat a compact risk label on relevant overview days and the full warning in\nthat day's timeline. Do not collapse warnings into optional fallback details.\n\nReview every surface for omissions: booking/payment uncertainty, station versus\nhotel arrival, early check-in, road closures, admission reservation, airport\nbuffers, outdated prices, and emergency contacts. This is a source review, not a\nrequirement to invent risks. Make selected/pending status, decision constraints,\nand budget uncertainty visually distinguishable from routine advice.\n\nUse `journey` for complicated transfers, showing each actual stage and a short\nqualifier. Use `practical-card` for city place and food checklists and booking actions;\nreserve tables for numerical comparison. Give emergency numbers direct `tel:`\nlinks where the source supports them. Chronological rows use a subtle timeline\nrail so the eye can follow time without colouring every item. The canonical\nstylesheet uses a measured page width, larger section headings, tabular time\nlabels and generous row spacing. Content surfaces remain opaque; glass stays\nin navigation. Phone timelines stack time above the activity while preserving\nthe rail and readable text size.\n\nLocalize navigation and dynamic control labels to the page language. Hotel-only\npages use `compact-hero`. Do not decorate every fact: priority actions, daily\nwarnings, and quiet explanatory content should have three distinct visual levels.\n\nWhen revising an existing trip, update the Markdown owner and companion HTML together, including overview, day timelines, both checklists, conditions and freshness notes. Scope claims of verification to facts actually rechecked.\n\nDuring refinement, open the actual generated pages, inspect desktop and phone\nlayouts in light and dark appearances, and iterate on the render. Check whether\na first-time reader can identify the journey, the next action, and the day's\nmain constraint without reading the entire page. Do not claim an objectively\n\"best\" UI; report what was inspected and any remaining verification gaps.\n\n## Build\n\nUse the deterministic Markdown renderer described in [render-source.md](render-source.md).\n\n```bash\nmake travel-build TRAVEL_DOC=data/travel/.md\nmake travel-check TRAVEL_DOC=data/travel/.md\n```\n\nThe renderer fills `templates/share.html`, then uses `scripts/build_share.py`\nonly as the canonical asset/control finalizer. Do not fill itinerary content\nmanually or run the low-level finalizer as a substitute for source rendering.\nKeep lodging cards inside the main page's `#stays`. Separate hotel pages have\nbeen retired and must not be recreated or linked.\n\nKeep the light background and glass navigation defaults, native appearance\ncheckboxes, offline embedded assets and print controls from the shared shell.\nA temporary script-free preview can be generated with the CLI's\n`--nojs-preview /private/tmp/travel-preview.html`; it is not another source.\n\nBuild/check validate balanced structure, canonical assets, classes, local\nanchors, checklist navigation, placeholders and day counts. They do not verify\ntravel facts, per-city completeness, browser rendering or colour contrast.\nInspect desktop/mobile renders when changing layout or claiming visual parity.\n\n## Component vocabulary\n\n| Class / element | Purpose |\n|---|---|\n| `.topbar` / `.topbar-inner` / `.nav` / `.brand` | Floating glass navigation |\n| `.tools` / `.icon-button.js-only` | Theme, reduced-glass, and print controls |\n| `.skip-link` | Keyboard route straight to main content |\n| `.hero` / `.eyebrow` / `.hero-sub` / `.dates` | Trip identity and dates |\n| `.facts` / `.route` / `.stop` | Compact | Optional short glass summary or native action link |\n| `.footer` / `.noprint` | Freshness note / screen-only controls |\n\nThe existing class vocabulary remains supported, so historical HTML does not\nrequire migration. Newly generated HTML must inline the current assets.\n\n## Mobile reading standard\n\nMobile is a deliberate composition, not a shrunken desktop grid. Use the\ncanonical screen-only breakpoint so printing remains a separate layout.\n\n- Match the web dashboard's compact top navigation and native appearance menu on\n desktop and phones. Keep section anchors visible, highlight the current section,\n and allow horizontal scrolling inside navigation when labels need it. Respect\n sticky-header offsets so destinations remain readable.\n- The first phone viewport should identify the trip, dates and next action.\n Avoid repeated destinations or large decorative summaries that delay useful content.\n- Let long day titles wrap, stack timelines on narrow screens and keep text\n readable. Practical contacts use a wrapping list; numeric comparisons may use\n a table with its own horizontal scroll region and visible hint.\n- Keep essential warnings expanded and visibly distinct. Optional explanations\n may collapse. Use 44px touch targets for standalone controls and map actions;\n inline prose links retain readable spacing. This is a design preference, not\n a claim about WCAG AA's minimum.\n- Verify narrow phones, a long day, dark appearance, reduced glass, keyboard focus\n and the document's final section. The full page should not scroll horizontally.\n\nReference checks: [WCAG target size](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/target-size-minimum.html)\nand [WCAG reflow](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/reflow.html).\nThese are engineering acceptance targets, not a blanket accessibility certification.\n\n## Visual acceptance\n\nUse the [shared acceptance check](../../../../docs/design-system.md#short-acceptance-check)\nfor the overall interface. For travel HTML, also inspect:\n\n- The first phone viewport, a long day, lodging, and a warning: trip identity,\n dates and next action remain clear, and sticky navigation does not cover anchors.\n- Phone widths around 390px and 320px: date/time labels wrap sensibly, and only\n intentional table or navigation regions need horizontal scrolling.\n- Theme, reduced-glass, section/map links and keyboard controls: they work and\n retain visible focus. Travel content remains available without JavaScript.\n- A4 print, including from dark mode: readable white surfaces, working map\n references and expanded fallback content.\n\nRecord environment limitations honestly. Structural validation checks the HTML\ncontract and asset parity; it does not prove these visual conditions. A bounded\ndocumentation or style change does not need a full model benchmark.\n\nVisual alignment check: compare the delivered HTML and the local dashboard at\nthe same viewport and zoom. ".agents/skills/travel-plan/references/share-html.md", "type": "text", "content": "# Standalone travel HTML\n\nA companion page rewrites the plan for the traveller; it is not a raw Markdown\nexport. Save it as `data/travel/-itinerary.html`. Hotel lists and other travel\nHTML derivatives use the same UI assets and can have their own page titles.\n\n## Content owner\n\nRead the latest trip Markdown before composing. Reconcile hotels, dates, nights,\nreturn transport, and booking status against it. Never reuse a stale HTML body as\ncurrent evidence. A restyle does not authorize changing the itinerary.\n\n| Drop | Keep | Add |\n|---|---|---|\n| Versions, changelog, research source appendix | Verified facts, constraints, maps, contact numbers | A one-sentence purpose per day |\n| Internal audit checklist and literal question-mark markers | The uncertainty itself | Plain instructions for confirming pending details |\n| Superseded arrangements | Day-specific fallbacks | One-line reasons for unusual routing |\n\nDistinguish selected hotels from paid bookings and target time windows from\nconfirmed services. Do not invent a quotation, room count, transport time, or\nbooking confirmation to fill a visual component.\n\n## Shared design guidance and travel composition\n\nRead [the shared interface design guide](../../../../docs/design-system.md)\nfor hierarchy, trustworthy information, material, accessibility and reuse.\nIt is the owner of those cross-page principles; this reference owns their travel\napplication, content semantics and offline HTML build.\n\nThe travel default uses the same neutral palette, typography scale, floating\nnavigation, appearance menu and card treatment as the local web dashboard,\nimplemented by the bundled assets. Adapt it when the user requests another direction. Start\nwith the traveller's immediate decisions: trip identity, dates, next action and\nimportant constraints. A date index leads into timelines; lodging can use comparison\ncards, transfers connected stages, and numerical costs tables. Use a compact\nintroduction for a hotel-only page. Preserve uncertainty and fallbacks while\nremoving repeated or decorative information.\n\nFor complete itineraries, read [checklists.md](checklists.md) and include separate city-by-city must-visit (`#places`) and must-eat (`#food`) sections, with top-navigation links, priority labels, actual dates/meals and maps. Keep all accepted places accounted for in daily routes, conditions or explicit unscheduled status.\n\nReuse `assets/share.css`, `assets/share.js` and `templates/share.html`; exact\nstyles and responsive behaviour stay there rather than becoming global rules.\nThe HTML is a CSS interpretation of glass, not Apple's native material. When\nchanging the shared visual treatment, update the canonical assets and reference\ntogether, then re-inline those assets into the affected outputs. Avoid per-trip\nCSS overrides: they drift from the next generated page.\n\n## Semantic hierarchy and action-first composition\n\nCalm styling must not flatten consequential information into ordinary notes.\nTranslate source markers by meaning, retaining uncertainty and severity:\n\n| Meaning | Treatment | Required text |\n|---|---|---|\n| High risk / source \ud83d\udea8 | Red `notice alert`, visible without expansion | Specific consequence and next action |\n| Hard constraint / source \u26a0\ufe0f | Amber `notice warning` | Constraint and what to confirm |\n| Pending / source \u2753 | Amber pending status; elevate to warning when it blocks travel | What is pending and how to resolve it |\n| Verified fact / source \u2705 | Quiet context, or `notice good` for a useful reassurance | Precisely what is verified |\n| Selected hotel | Green `status selected` | Selected; never imply paid or booked without evidence |\n| Ordinary explanation | Neutral `note` or blue `notice info` | Brief context, not an alarm |\n\nUse `notice-icon`, `notice-title`, and `notice-copy` to make callouts readable.\nIcons are supplementary (`aria-hidden=\"true\"`); meaningful labels and actions\nmust remain in text. Colour alone is insufficient. Keep long copy in normal ink.\n\nWhen the source has pre-departure blockers, put a prominent amber `priority`\nsection immediately after the trip introduction, with a concise action heading\nand an `action-grid` of short `action-card` items. Show red urgent labels only on\nitems whose source supports that severity. Prefer human instructions and one\nclear action per item over raw field/value lists. Do not add invented deadlines.\nThe canonical renderer promotes marked timeline notes from muted metadata to\nexpanded callouts. Keep urgent row and overview labels red; amber is for\nconstraints or pending facts. The hero action links to the existing priority\nsection, which keeps its amber outline and source-supported red inner warnings.\nRepeat a compact risk label on relevant overview days and the full warning in\nthat day's timeline. Do not collapse warnings into optional fallback details.\n\nReview every surface for omissions: booking/payment uncertainty, station versus\nhotel arrival, early check-in, road closures, admission reservation, airport\nbuffers, outdated prices, and emergency contacts. This is a source review, not a\nrequirement to invent risks. Make selected/pending status, decision constraints,\nand budget uncertainty visually distinguishable from routine advice.\n\nUse `journey` for complicated transfers, showing each actual stage and a short\nqualifier. Use `practical-card` for city place and food checklists and booking actions;\nreserve tables for numerical comparison. Give emergency numbers direct `tel:`\nlinks where the source supports them. Chronological rows use a subtle timeline\nrail so the eye can follow time without colouring every item.\n\nLocalize navigation and dynamic control labels to the page language. Hotel-only\npages use `compact-hero`. Do not decorate every fact: priority actions, daily\nwarnings, and quiet explanatory content should have three distinct visual levels.\n\nWhen revising an existing trip, update the Markdown owner and companion HTML together, including overview, day timelines, both checklists, conditions and freshness notes. Scope claims of verification to facts actually rechecked.\n\nDuring refinement, open the actual generated pages, inspect desktop and phone\nlayouts in light and dark appearances, and iterate on the render. Check whether\na first-time reader can identify the journey, the next action, and the day's\nmain constraint without reading the entire page. Do not claim an objectively\n\"best\" UI; report what was inspected and any remaining verification gaps.\n\n## Build\n\nUse the deterministic Markdown renderer described in [render-source.md](render-source.md).\n\n```bash\nmake travel-build TRAVEL_DOC=data/travel/.md\nmake travel-check TRAVEL_DOC=data/travel/.md\n```\n\nThe renderer fills `templates/share.html`, then uses `scripts/build_share.py`\nonly as the canonical asset/control finalizer. Do not fill itinerary content\nmanually or run the low-level finalizer as a substitute for source rendering.\nKeep lodging cards inside the main page's `#stays`. Separate hotel pages have\nbeen retired and must not be recreated or linked.\n\nKeep the light background and glass navigation defaults, native appearance\ncheckboxes, offline embedded assets and print controls from the shared shell.\nA temporary script-free preview can be generated with the CLI's\n`--nojs-preview /private/tmp/travel-preview.html`; it is not another source.\n\nBuild/check validate balanced structure, canonical assets, classes, local\nanchors, checklist navigation, placeholders and day counts. They do not verify\ntravel facts, per-city completeness, browser rendering or colour contrast.\nInspect desktop/mobile renders when changing layout or claiming visual parity.\n\n## Component vocabulary\n\n| Class / element | Purpose |\n|---|---|\n| `.topbar` / `.topbar-inner` / `.nav` / `.brand` | Floating glass navigation |\n| `.tools` / `.icon-button.js-only` | Theme, reduced-glass, and print controls |\n| `.skip-link` | Keyboard route straight to main content |\n| `.hero` / `.eyebrow` / `.hero-sub` / `.dates` | Trip identity and dates |\n| `.facts` / `.route` / `.stop` | Compact facts and destination summaries |\n| `.overview` | Clickable day overview cards linking to real day IDs |\n| `.grid` / `.hotel` / `.hotel-meta` / `.hotel-links` | Responsive lodging cards |\n| `.status.selected` / `.status.pending` / `.status.urgent` | Selected, pending, and urgent labels |\n| `.priority` / `.action-grid` / `.action-card` | Prominent pre-departure actions |\n| `.notice.warning` / `.notice.alert` / `.notice.info` / `.notice.good` | Amber constraint, red risk, blue context, green verified fact |\n| `.notice-icon` / `.notice-title` / `.notice-copy` | Supplementary icon, visible label, readable instruction |\n| `.journey` | Ordered transfer stages with short qualifiers |\n| `.practical-card` / `.contact-grid` / `.contact-link` | Human-readable food/booking cards and direct emergency calls |\n| `.risk-label` / `.hero-action` / `.compact-hero` | Overview warning, next-action link, compact derivative introduction |\n| `.section-head` / `.sub` / `.panel` | Section heading, supporting line, content surface |\n| `.day-nav` | Native date anchor navigation |\n| `.day` / `.dayhead` / `.daynum` / `.daydate` / `.daytitle` / `.daylede` | One chronological day |\n| `.sched` > `li.key` > `.time` + `.what` with `.meta` | Timeline and important rows |\n| `.map` | One map provider link immediately after a place name |\n| `.tag` / `.tag.free` / `.tag.warn` | Costs and readable status labels |\n| `.alert` / `.note` / `.good` with `.t` | Critical constraints, context/fallbacks, upsides |\n| `.scroll` around `table`, optionally `.tot` | Contained table scrolling on phones |\n| `.kv` (`dl`, `dt`, `dd`) | Practical information pairs |\n| `.plain` / `.tight` / `.n` | Existing list and numeric-cell compatibility |\n| `.glass` / `.button` | Optional short glass summary or native action link |\n| `.footer` / `.noprint` | Freshness note / screen-only controls |\n\nThe existing class vocabulary remains supported, so historical HTML does not\nrequire migration. Newly generated HTML must inline the current assets.\n\n## Mobile reading standard\n\nMobile is a deliberate composition, not a shrunken desktop grid. Use the\ncanonical screen-only breakpoint so printing remains a separate layout.\n\n- Match the web dashboard's compact top navigation and native appearance menu on\n desktop and phones. Keep section anchors visible, highlight the current section,\n and allow horizontal scrolling inside navigation when labels need it. Respect\n sticky-header offsets so destinations remain readable.\n- The first phone viewport should identify the trip, dates and next action.\n Avoid repeated destinations or large decorative summaries that delay useful content.\n- Let long day titles wrap, stack timelines on narrow screens and keep text\n readable. Practical contacts use a wrapping list; numeric comparisons may use\n a table with its own horizontal scroll region and visible hint.\n- Keep essential warnings expanded and visibly distinct. Optional explanations\n may collapse. Use 44px touch targets for standalone controls and map actions;\n inline prose links retain readable spacing. This is a design preference, not\n a claim about WCAG AA's minimum.\n- Verify narrow phones, a long day, dark appearance, reduced glass, keyboard focus\n and the document's final section. The full page should not scroll horizontally.\n\nReference checks: [WCAG target size](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/target-size-minimum.html)\nand [WCAG reflow](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/reflow.html).\nThese are engineering acceptance targets, not a blanket accessibility certification.\n\n## Visual acceptance\n\nUse the [shared acceptance check](../../../../docs/design-system.md#short-acceptance-check)\nfor the overall interface. For travel HTML, also inspect:\n\n- The first phone viewport, a long day, lodging, and a warning: trip identity,\n dates and next action remain clear, and sticky navigation does not cover anchors.\n- Phone widths around 390px and 320px: date/time labels wrap sensibly, and only\n intentional table or navigation regions need horizontal scrolling.\n- Theme, reduced-glass, section/map links and keyboard controls: they work and\n retain visible focus. Travel content remains available without JavaScript.\n- A4 print, including from dark mode: readable white surfaces, working map\n references and expanded fallback content.\n\nRecord environment limitations honestly. Structural validation checks the HTML\ncontract and asset parity; it does not prove these visual conditions. A bounded\ndocumentation or style change does not need a full model benchmark.\n\nVisual alignment check: compare the delivered HTML and the local dashboard at\nthe same viewport and zoom. Check typography, spacing, surfaces and navigation\nas well as icons; a matching icon family alone does not establish consistency.\n\n## Icons\n\nUse the bundled Lucide SVG assets for every HTML status/navigation icon.\nNo visible emoji in HTML. Keep semantic markers in Markdown for lint and risk\nclassification; `scripts/lib/travel/icons.py` converts text markers during render,\npreserving links, canonical CSS/JS and accompanying labels. "type": "text", "content": "{\n \"input.json\": \"bad581e18529407529cf011ca4c94be7c0ae1539a71d68c608680cddd731e354\",\n \"result.json\": \"b8e26874d3a9feeaf777d1d4a7d41aec515989f93dba957ed1f7e4fd5d02feb2\",\n \"fixture-before/hotel.html\": \"9978a36dafef18846c6e2debebae46616d6f60cc76673da82d9946ad685202cc\",\n \"fixture-before/itinerary.html\": \"f7241bf3496b1f31beb4667b874362d1a6e356513c17e2bd1e9c66aa4f1181b7\",\n \"fixture-before/plan.md\": \"752b9f532920a1c159e51fc4077edad9cb3635371a550d618801460037149b90\"\n}\n"}, {"name": ".agents/skills/travel-plan/SKILL.md", "type": "text", "content": "---\nname: travel-plan\ndescription: Produces and revises trip plans under data/travel/ at the repo's v4 standard on the first pass \u2014 a verification gate before any day is written, per-day timeline tables with dual map links, marker discipline (\u2705/\u26a0\ufe0f/\u2753/\ud83d\udea8), city-by-city must-visit and must-eat checklists, and a companion itinerary HTML build. Use this skill whenever the user asks to plan, draft, review, revise, or \"make more detailed\" a trip, itinerary, travel plan, or \u884c\u7a0b/\u65c5\u884c\u8ba1\u5212, or mentions a file under data/travel/. Also use it when turning an existing plan into an itinerary page for a travel companion.\n---\n\n# Trip Plans\n\nThe repo already has two labelled records of what goes wrong: `tests/fixtures/travel_known_defects.yaml` (47 defects from the Shanghai plan's v2\u2192v4 changelog, plus the Changsha v1\u2192v4 round). Read the ratio before you start:\n\n| class | count | who catches it |\n|---|---|---|\n| **verify** | 22+ | **nobody, unless you look it up before writing** |\n| lint | 15+ | `make travel-lint`, mechanically |\n| adjudicate / judgment / process | 10 | a human |\n\n**Verify-class defects are the reason plans take four versions.** They are not stylistic \u2014 they are facts that were wrong, and no amount of re-reading the draft surfaces them. A plan that skips \u00a71 and goes straight to writing days will be a v1 that needs a v4.\n\nSo the order is fixed: **verify, then write.** Not write, then review.\n\n## Record preservation\n\nKeep the Markdown source as the durable record of versions, corrections, source\nlinks, verification dates and unresolved rechecks; HTML is the traveller-facing\nitinerary. Edit trip content only in Markdown; regenerate the main itinerary HTML for revisions. A request to simplify the\nvisible deliverable does not imply that unique audit information may be discarded.\nBefore deleting or retiring a source file, compare its records with the retained\nartifact, migrate unique records and preserve a recoverable copy. If a record is\nrestored from an older backup or reconstructed, label its recovery scope and gaps;\ndo not present a reconstructed file as an exact original.\n\n## Workflow\n\n0. **Locate** \u2014 for revisions, find and read the existing trip plan and companion itinerary before asking the traveller to resend them. Search the known `data/travel/` location, including ignored private data. Ask only for information that is still unavailable.\n1. **Gate** \u2014 run the \u00a71 verification sweep. Do not write a single day until it is done.\n2. **Compose** \u2014 `templates/plan.md` + the structure contract in `references/structure.md`. Read [references/checklists.md](references/checklists.md) for the city-by-city must-visit and must-eat lists, priority labels, evidence and schedule coverage.\n3. **Self-review** \u2014 the \u00a73 pass.\n4. **Lint** \u2014 `make travel-lint` must be clean before you hand it over.\n5. **Generate HTML** \u2014 read [references/render-source.md](references/render-source.md) for the schema and deterministic workflow. Markdown is the sole editable trip content. Run `make travel-build TRAVEL_DOC=data/travel/.md`, then `make travel-check TRAVEL_DOC=data/travel/.md`. The builder lints the source, renders the shared template/CSS/JS, and validates the complete page before replacement. It makes no AI/network calls. Do not independently author generated itinerary content. HTML uses only bundled Lucide SVG icons: never emit emoji glyphs. Markdown status markers remain semantic inputs; the renderer converts visible markers while retaining text labels. Keep lodging in `#stays` on the main page; never create a separate hotel HTML or link to one. For all existing trips use `make travel-build-all` and `make travel-check-all`. For older sources, normalize explicitly before building; retain verification dates and recovery gaps. For shared UI work, read [the shared interface design guide](../../../docs/design-system.md) and `references/share-html.md`; inspect actual desktop/mobile renders before claiming visual alignment.\n\nWrite the source plan to `data/travel/-YYYY-MM.md` and the companion HTML to `data/travel/-YYYY-MM-itinerary.html`. Use `itinerary` consistently in output filenames and cross-page links. Chinese is fine here and usually correct \u2014 `data/travel/` is the documented language exception in AGENTS.md, because map queries, mini-program names and restaurant names have to stay in the local script to be usable on the ground.\n\n---\n\n## 1. The verification gate\n\nNine categories. Every one of them has produced a real defect in the corpus. Run them **before** drafting, and carry the answers into the draft as \u2705 lines with sources.\n\nIf a lookup comes back empty, that is a result \u2014 mark it \u2753 and say what would settle it (a phone number, an official page, \"check at the counter\"). **A \u2753 you declared is fine. A fact you assumed is not.**\n\n### 1.1 Calendar collisions \u2014 for every date in range, in every city\n\nPublic holidays, festivals, conventions, marathons, expos. Check the **national holiday notice** for the travel year, not a generic \"when is X\" answer \u2014 holidays move, and compensatory workdays move with them.\n\n> Shanghai v3 moved the whole trip a week for \u8fdb\u535a\u4f1a. Changsha v1\u2013v3 missed \u4e2d\u79cb entirely: three of twelve days sat inside a public holiday nobody had noticed, which changes ticket availability, room rates and station crowds.\n\nCross-check the holiday range against the day table and say, per affected day, what it means.\n\n### 1.2 Venue closure days\n\nMonday closures for museums, seasonal closures, maintenance days. The corpus calls closure-day collisions \"the single most mechanical defect\" \u2014 and it is still verify-class, because it needs a per-venue lookup.\n\n### 1.3 Operating status of every mechanical conveyance\n\nCable cars, gondolas, lifts, funiculars, escalators, ferries, sightseeing trains. **These go out for months at a time and every blog post predates the outage.**\n\n> Changsha v1\u2013v3 routed a whole day through a cable car whose upper section had been closed for renovation for ten months. The route, the ticket price and the day's shape all changed once that was checked.\n\nAsk specifically: is it running *right now*, and what is the fallback route when it is not?\n\n### 1.4 \"There is no direct route\" claims\n\nBefore writing a long bus transfer or a multi-leg workaround, check whether a nearby station solves it. Do not inherit the claim from a guide.\n\n> Changsha v1\u2013v3 wrote a 3\u20134 h intercity bus with unverifiable departures, calling it \"the only segment with no timetable\". A high-speed station 30 km away turned it into a bookable 1 h train \u2014 on a road the itinerary already traversed two days earlier.\n\n### 1.5 Booking windows\n\nAdvance-sale periods decide the whole booking calendar. Find the window (in days, and whether it includes the travel date), then compute the actual on-sale dates.\n\n> 12306 is 15 days including the travel date \u2014 which made a single \"book everything on 10/17\" checkpoint infeasible and forced a four-date calendar. Panda-base tickets have two published windows (7 days, and 14 days including today) that disagree; the plan says both and names the hard one.\n\n### 1.6 First and last service times at the edges of the day\n\nAny leg that lands before ~07:00 or after ~21:00. Airport rail, metro, maglev, shuttle buses all stop earlier than people assume.\n\n> A 01:10 landing means the maglev and the airport bus are both long finished \u2014 taxi is the only option, and that changes the arrival timeline and cost.\n\n### 1.7 Ticket composition \u2014 what a combo ticket actually contains\n\nNever write \"combo ticket \u00a5X\" without listing what is inside it and what is still extra.\n\n> Changsha v1\u2013v3 treated a \u00a5227 gate ticket and a \u00a5238 cable-car pass as two tiers of the same thing. They are different tickets: the gate ticket already includes the shuttle bus; the cable cars are never included. Getting this wrong either overspends or strands you.\n\n### 1.8 Lodging-night arithmetic\n\nCount nights, not days. Then check the two traps:\n\n- **After-midnight arrival** \u2014 a 01:10 landing means the first room is booked for **the previous calendar date**. Booking it on the arrival date puts you in the lobby at 3am.\n- **After-midnight departure** \u2014 the mirror image: the ticket says the next day, the traveller must be at the airport the evening before.\n\nState the first and last night explicitly. `make travel-lint` checks both traps, but only if the dates are on the page.\n\n### 1.9 Door-to-door time, not in-vehicle time\n\nA transfer day's duration is station access + wait + ride + transfer + ride + onward access. Summing only the rides understates it by hours.\n\n> \"The whole trip is about 3.7 h including the transfer\" was 2h58m + 40m with nothing else counted. Door-to-door was ~5.5 h. `make travel-lint` now catches a stated total that is less than the legs it sits next to.\n\n---\n\n## 2. What the document must contain\n\nFull contract in `references/structure.md`. The short version:\n\n- **Header** \u2014 version, re-verification deadline, **the verification date all data is stated as of**, marker legend.\n- **A \"do this now\" block** \u2014 the two or three things that cannot be recovered if missed. Ranked by consequence, not by document order.\n- **\u00a70 one-page overview** \u2014 day table, plus explicit totals (days / stops / nights) that agree with each other.\n- **\u00a71 basics** \u00b7 **\u00a72 pre-departure, back-dated from deadlines** \u00b7 **\u00a73 the anchor activities**\n- **\u00a74 day-by-day** \u2014 this is the body of the document, see below; **\u00a74.1 must-visit checklist** follows the daily timelines.\n- **\u00a75 must-eat checklist** \u00b7 **\u00a76 pitfalls** \u00b7 **\u00a77 budget** \u00b7 **\u00a78 packing** \u00b7 **\u00a79 emergency** \u00b7 **\u00a710 re-verify checklist** \u00b7 **\u00a711 changelog** \u00b7 **\u00a712 sources**\n\n### The per-day format\n\nEvery day gets: a `> **\u5b9a\u4f4d\uff1a**` one-line judgement of what the day *is*, then a five-column table, then any fallback blocks.\n\n```markdown\n### Day 4\uff08\u5468\u4e94 9/25\uff09\ud83d\udea8 \u4e2d\u79cb\u5f53\u5929 \u00b7 \u6b66\u9675\u6e90\u6838\u5fc3\u666f\u533a\u5168\u5929\n\n> **\u5b9a\u4f4d\uff1a\u5168\u5929\u4e00\u4e2a\u666f\u533a\uff0c\u4e0d\u8d70\u56de\u5934\u8def\u3002**\n> \ud83d\udea8 \u4e2d\u79cb\u5047\u671f\u7b2c\u4e00\u5929 \u2014\u2014 07:00 \u5c31\u5230\u95e8\u7968\u7ad9\uff0c\u665a\u4e00\u5c0f\u65f6\u4f1a\u591a\u6392\u534a\u5c0f\u65f6\u3002\n\n| \u65f6\u95f4 | \u5b89\u6392 | \u5730\u56fe | \u95e8\u7968 | \u5907\u6ce8 |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| **07:00** | \u5230[\u5434\u5bb6\u5cea\u53e3\u95e8\u7968\u7ad9](\u9ad8\u5fb7\u94fe\u63a5) \u00b7 [G](google\u94fe\u63a5) | \u540c\u4e0a | **\u00a5227** \u2705 | \u542b\u4fdd\u9669/\u542b\u73af\u4fdd\u8f66/4\u5929\u6709\u6548 |\n```\n\nRules that make the format worth the effort:\n\n- **Times are derived, not invented.** Build each day by chaining verified durations from a fixed anchor. If a duration is unknown, the time inherits \u2753.\n- **Dual map links on first mention** of every place \u2014 \u9ad8\u5fb7 + Google. One of the two will be unreachable depending on the traveller's network; the pair is the redundancy.\n- **Every day that can fail gets a fallback block** \u2014 weather, sold out, closed, too tired. Put it under that day, not in a distant appendix.\n- **Fixed costs are in the \u95e8\u7968 column**, so the budget can be reconciled against the days.\n\n### Marker discipline\n\n| | meaning |\n|---|---|\n| \u2705 | verified against an official or strong source \u2014 **and the source is in \u00a712** |\n| \u26a0\ufe0f | a constraint or trap that changes behaviour |\n| \u2753 | unverified, conflicting sources, or \"ask on the day\" \u2014 **name what would settle it** |\n| \ud83d\udea8 | high consequence: missing it costs the trip, not a detour |\n\nNever leave a specific departure time bare. `unsourced_specific` fires on any precise time near a departure word without \u2705 / \u2753 / a link \u2014 that rule exists because bare times read as verified when they were estimated.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Self-review before handing over\n\nRun these against the draft. They are the failure modes that survived a generation pass in the corpus.\n\n1. **Every date in range** \u2014 checked against the holiday calendar? (\u00a71.1)\n2. **Every conveyance** \u2014 operating status checked? (\u00a71.3)\n3. **Every combo ticket** \u2014 contents enumerated? (\u00a71.7)\n4. **Nights** \u2014 do the day table, the totals line, and the budget all use the same number? Is the first night's date right? (\u00a71.8)\n5. **Every transfer day** \u2014 door-to-door, or just the rides? (\u00a71.9)\n6. **Every \u2753** \u2014 does it say what would settle it, and is it in the \u00a710 re-verify checklist?\n7. **Every \u2705** \u2014 is its source in \u00a712?\n8. **Overclaims** \u2014 \u5b8c\u5168 / \u7edd\u5bf9 / \u4e00\u5b9a / \u4ece\u4e0d on an unsourced line is almost always wrong. The corpus has one: \"sells no tickets on site at all\" \u2014 a foreign-visitor counter existed.\n9. **The summary table vs the days** \u2014 lodging column, durations, and totals must agree. Lint catches some of this; read it anyway.\n10. **Checklist coverage** \u2014 every accepted place has a day/route, a conditional fallback, or an explicit unscheduled status; every priority food has a meal/area. Reconcile the source plan, checklist cards, day links and companion HTML. Structural validation cannot verify this semantic agreement.\n\nThen: `make travel-lint`. **A plan that does not lint clean is not finished.**\n\n## 4. Revising an existing plan\n\nSame gate. A revision request (\"make it more detailed\", \"review this\") is **not** permission to skip \u00a71 \u2014 detail added on top of unverified facts is worse than a thin correct plan, because it reads as authoritative.\n\n- Bump the version, add a changelog row saying **what was wrong**, not just what changed.\n- Never silently delete a wrong claim \u2014 the changelog is how the user knows not to trust their memory of v1.\n- Revise routes by chaining nearby places and allowing door-to-door transfers, meals, rest, luggage collection and station buffers. A new place uses real available time; do not merely append it to an already full day. Keep conditional visits visibly conditional, and identify what is replaced or still unscheduled.\n- Edit changed days, overview, city checklists, warnings/fallbacks and freshness notes in Markdown, then regenerate and check the existing itinerary HTML; do not report a chat-only proposal as saved. For a bounded revision, state which facts were newly checked and which prior verification dates remain in force.\n- Keep the changelog section excluded from lint (it quotes superseded values on purpose; `_excluded_mask` already handles this).\n- When a revision finds defects worth remembering, add them to `tests/fixtures/travel_known_defects.yaml` with a `class` \u2014 that file is the eval set for this skill.\n\n## 5. "content": "# Travel Markdown \u2192 HTML build\n\n[Status: OK] Design-Lite \u00b7 2026-10-06\n\n## 1. Problem and outcome\n\nTrips currently require independent Markdown and HTML edits. The source holds\nversion history and evidence that cannot safely be discarded, while independently\nrewritten HTML can omit a warning or retain an obsolete route. Keep Markdown as\nthe only editable trip content and generate the existing standalone HTML UI.\n\n## Latest approved delivery decision\n\nThe initial HTML-only request is superseded: retain Markdown when it contains\nnecessary records and use deterministic Markdown-to-HTML generation. Edit trip\ncontent in Markdown, then run `scripts/travel_build.py` and its `--check` mode.\nLodging belongs in the main itinerary; do not generate a separate hotel page.\nVisible HTML icons use bundled Lucide SVG with visible text labels, never emoji.\nMarkdown markers remain semantic inputs; builds do not refresh factual evidence.\n\n## 2. Scope and ownership\n\n| Owner | Consumer | Change |\n|---|---|---|\n| Trip Markdown | New strict parser and renderer | Small opt-in `travel_render` schema and named current-stays table |\n| Canonical travel HTML template/CSS/JS | Renderer and existing HTML finalizer | Reuse styles, timelines, cards and native controls |\n| Renderer | `scripts/travel_build.py`, Makefile | Build, stale check, optional no-script preview |\n| Travel skill/references | Future agents | AI plans/verifies/edits MD; scripts render/check HTML |\n\nNo network or AI runs in the builder. Existing legacy sources/pages remain\nreadable; unsupported source formats fail rather than being rewritten silently.\nOnly the main itinerary is generated; separate hotel HTML pages are retired.\nThe first migration uses the current trip. Other sources require explicit\nnormalization, particularly the incomplete recovered source.\n\n## 3. Flow\n\n```text\nAI/user \u2192 Markdown [MODIFIED: sole editable content]\n \u2193\n strict parser + source lint [NEW / EXISTING]\n \u2193\n structured blocks in memory [NEW, no persisted second owner]\n \u2193\n timeline/stay/checklist renderer [NEW]\n \u2193\n canonical shell + HTML validation [EXISTING]\n \u2193\n Main itinerary HTML, including lodging [GENERATED]\n```\n\n## 4. Input and output contract\n\n`travel_render` YAML identifies schema 1, language and optional display title.\nRequire numbered sections 0\u201312 and 4.1, a five-column overview, consecutive\n`Day N` headings with purpose quotes and five-column timeline tables, city\nchecklist tables, and one explicitly marked current-stays table with status.\nPreserve other prose, tables, map/source links and review/history sections.\n\nCurrent stay states are explicit (`\u5df2\u9009`, `\u5f85\u786e\u8ba4`, `\u5df2\u9884\u8ba2`); `\u5df2\u9009` never means\npaid. Hotel date ranges must reconcile with stated nights. Unknown keys, malformed\ntables, unsupported markup, missing sections, invalid links and ambiguous maps\nraise source-line errors. Plain text/Markdown is escaped, not executed as HTML.\n\nOverview, daily headings and dates agree. Source marker meaning is retained:\ncritical/constraint/pending notices stay visible, verified context stays quiet.\nCity cards link dates to real day anchors. Sources, versions, evidence dates and\nreview tasks remain in expandable supporting sections in the generated page.\n\nOutput is deterministic and carries source filename/hash and schema version.\n`--check` rebuilds in memory and compares all requested outputs without writing;\nchanges in Markdown, renderer, template or assets make it stale. Validation occurs\nbefore replacing output. Source files cannot be output/preview paths. A malformed\nsource must leave existing outputs intact.\n\n## 5. AI and script responsibilities\n\n| Trigger | Action |\n|---|---|\n| Add/remove stops, change priorities, replan transfers | AI verifies and edits source MD, then builds |\n| Explicit known booking/status correction | User/AI edits the source field; build |\n| Source unchanged, style/template update | Build without AI |\n| Parser cannot understand a source | AI/user normalizes the reported line; no automatic guessing |\n| Opening/pricing/closure evidence stale | AI checks sources; build is not factual verification |\n| Shared UI changes or visual defects | Inspect actual desktop/mobile render; fix canonical renderer/assets |\n\n## 6. Acceptance and risks\n\nSynthetic tests cover content/link/history preservation, risk states, day/stay\nagreement, malformed input, unsafe links, reproducibility, stale detection and\nno-overwrite on failure. Run existing source lint and HTML/controls checks plus\none real local trip build. Browser availability is reported separately from\nstructural checks; no claim of visual equivalence without actual renders.\n\nThe intentional trade-off is a bounded Markdown grammar rather than arbitrary\nHTML passthrough. This avoids new runtime dependencies and prevents silently\nflattening unrecognized content. Do not broaden it by guessing: extend its\ncontract and tests when an actual source needs another supported construct.\n\n## 7. self.raw = None\n def handle_starttag(self, tag, attrs):\n self.parts.append(self.get_starttag_text())\n if tag in {'script', 'style', 'title'}: self.raw = tag\n def handle_startendtag(self, tag, attrs): self.parts.append(self.get_starttag_text())\n def handle_endtag(self, tag):\n self.parts.append(f'')\n if tag == self.raw: self.raw = None\n def handle_data(self, data):\n if self.raw == 'title':\n self.parts.append(EMOJI.sub('', data)); return\n if self.raw: self.parts.append(data); return\n def replace(match):\n key = match[0].replace('\\ufe0f', '').replace('\\ufe0e', '')\n name, label = MARKERS.get(key, ('Info', '\u63d0\u793a'))\n icon = (skill / 'assets/icons' / (name + '.svg')).read_text().strip()\n return '' + icon + '' + label + ''\n self.parts.append(EMOJI.sub(replace, data))\n def handle_entityref(self, name): self.parts.append(f'&{name};')\n def handle_charref(self, name):\n value = int(name[1:], 16) if name.lower().startswith('x') else int(name)\n char = chr(value)\n if EMOJI.search(char): self.handle_data(char)\n else: self.parts.append(f'&#{name};')\n def handle_comment(self, text): self.parts.append('')\n def handle_decl(self, text): self.parts.append('')\n parser = Convert(); '; content.innerHTML = html; } function toggleGrades() { const content = document.getElementById("grades-content"); const arrow = document.getElementById("grades-arrow"); content.classList.toggle("open"); arrow.classList.toggle("open"); } // ---- Previous outputs (collapsible) ---- function renderPrevOutputs(run) { const section = document.getElementById("prev-outputs-section"); const content = document.getElementById("prev-outputs-content"); const prevOutputs = (EMBEDDED_DATA.previous_outputs || {})[run.id]; if (!prevOutputs || prevOutputs.length === 0) { section.style.display = "none"; return; } section.style.display = "block"; // Reset to collapsed content.classList.remove("open"); document.getElementById("prev-outputs-arrow").classList.remove("open"); // Render the files into the content area content.innerHTML = ""; const wrapper = document.createElement("div"); wrapper.style.padding = "1rem"; for (const file of prevOutputs) { const fileDiv = document.createElement("div"); fileDiv.className = "output-file"; const header = document.createElement("div"); header.className = "output-file-header"; const nameSpan = document.createElement("span"); nameSpan.textContent = file.name; header.appendChild(nameSpan); const dlBtn = document.createElement("a"); dlBtn.className = "dl-btn"; dlBtn.textContent = "Download"; dlBtn.download = file.name; dlBtn.href = getDownloadUri(file); header.appendChild(dlBtn); fileDiv.appendChild(header); const fc = document.createElement("div"); fc.className = "output-file-content"; if (file.type === "text") { const pre = document.createElement("pre"); pre.textContent = file.content; fc.appendChild(pre); } else if (file.type === "image") { const img = document.createElement("img"); img.src = file.data_uri; img.alt = file.name; fc.appendChild(img); } else if (file.type === "pdf") { const iframe = document.createElement("iframe"); iframe.src = file.data_uri; fc.appendChild(iframe); } else if (file.type === "xlsx") { renderXlsx(fc, file.data_b64); } else if (file.type === "binary") { const a = document.createElement("a"); a.className = "download-link"; a.href = file.data_uri; a.download = file.name; a.textContent = "Download " + file.name; fc.appendChild(a); } fileDiv.appendChild(fc); wrapper.appendChild(fileDiv); } content.appendChild(wrapper); } function togglePrevOutputs() { const content = document.getElementById("prev-outputs-content"); const arrow = document.getElementById("prev-outputs-arrow"); content.classList.toggle("open"); arrow.classList.toggle("open"); } // ---- Feedback (saved to server -> feedback.json) ---- function saveCurrentFeedback() { const run = EMBEDDED_DATA.runs[currentIndex]; const text = document.getElementById("feedback").value; if (text.trim() === "") { delete feedbackMap[run.id]; } else { feedbackMap[run.id] = text; } // Build reviews array from map const reviews = []; for (const [run_id, feedback] of Object.entries(feedbackMap)) { if (feedback.trim()) { reviews.push({ run_id, feedback, timestamp: new Date().toISOString() }); } } fetch("/api/feedback", { method: "POST", headers: { "Content-Type": "application/json" }, body: JSON.stringify({ reviews, status: "in_progress" }), }).then(() => { document.getElementById("feedback-status").textContent = "Saved"; }).catch(() => { // Static mode or server unavailable — no-op on auto-save, // feedback will be downloaded on final submit document.getElementById("feedback-status").textContent = "Will download on submit"; }); } // ---- Done ---- function showDoneDialog() { // Save current textarea to feedbackMap (but don't POST yet) const run = EMBEDDED_DATA.runs[currentIndex]; const text = document.getElementById("feedback").value; if (text.trim() === "") { delete feedbackMap[run.id]; } else { feedbackMap[run.id] = text; } // POST once with status: complete — include ALL runs so the model // can distinguish "no feedback" (looks good) from "not reviewed" const reviews = []; const ts = new Date().toISOString(); for (const r of EMBEDDED_DATA.runs) { reviews.push({ run_id: r.id, feedback: feedbackMap[r.id] || "", timestamp: ts }); } const payload = JSON.stringify({ reviews, status: "complete" }, null, 2); fetch("/api/feedback", { method: "POST", headers: { "Content-Type": "application/json" }, body: payload, }).then(() => { document.getElementById("done-overlay").classList.add("visible"); }).catch(() => { // Server not available (static mode) — download as file const blob = new Blob([payload], { type: "application/json" }); const url = URL.createObjectURL(blob); const a = document.createElement("a"); a.href = url; a.download = "feedback.json"; a.click(); URL.revokeObjectURL(url); document.getElementById("done-overlay").classList.add("visible"); }); } function closeDoneDialog() { // Reset status back to in_progress saveCurrentFeedback(); document.getElementById("done-overlay").classList.remove("visible"); } // ---- Toast ---- function showToast(message) { const toast = document.getElementById("toast"); toast.textContent = message; toast.classList.add("visible"); setTimeout(() => toast.classList.remove("visible"), 2000); } // ---- Keyboard nav ---- document.addEventListener("keydown", (e) => { // Don't capture when typing in textarea if (e.target.tagName === "TEXTAREA") return; if (e.key === "ArrowLeft" || e.key === "ArrowUp") { e.preventDefault(); navigate(-1); } else if (e.key === "ArrowRight" || e.key === "ArrowDown") { e.preventDefault(); navigate(1); } }); // ---- Util ---- function getDownloadUri(file) { if (file.data_uri) return file.data_uri; if (file.data_b64) return "data:application/octet-stream;base64," + file.data_b64; if (file.type === "text") return "data:text/plain;charset=utf-8," + encodeURIComponent(file.content); return "#"; } function escapeHtml(text) { const div = document.createElement("div"); div.textContent = text; return div.innerHTML; } // ---- View switching ---- function switchView(view) { document.querySelectorAll(".view-tab").forEach(t => t.classList.remove("active")); document.querySelectorAll(".view-panel").forEach(p => p.classList.remove("active")); document.querySelector(`[onclick="switchView('${view}')"]`).classList.add("active"); document.getElementById("panel-" + view).classList.add("active"); } // ---- Benchmark rendering ---- function renderBenchmark() { const data = EMBEDDED_DATA.benchmark; "; html += "

"; // Summary table html += ''; function fmtStat(stat, pct) { if (!stat) return "—"; const suffix = pct ? "%" : ""; const m = pct ? (stat.mean * 100).toFixed(0) : stat.mean.toFixed(1); const s = pct ? (stat.stddev * 100).toFixed(0) : stat.stddev.toFixed(1); return m + suffix + " ± " + s + suffix; } function deltaClass(val) { if (!val) return ""; const n = parseFloat(val); if (n > 0) return "benchmark-delta-positive"; if (n < 0) return "benchmark-delta-negative"; return ""; } // Discover config names dynamically (everything except "delta") const configs = Object.keys(summary).filter(k => k !== "delta"); const configA = configs[0] || "config_a"; const configB = configs[1] || "config_b"; const labelA = configA.replace(/_/g, " ").replace(/\b\w/g, c => c.toUpperCase()); const labelB = configB.replace(/_/g, " ").replace(/\b\w/g, c => c.toUpperCase()); const a = summary[configA] || {}; const b = summary[configB] || {}; const delta = summary.delta || {}; html += ""; html += ""; html += ""; html += ""; html += ""; html += '"; // Time (only show row if data exists) if (a.time_seconds || b.time_seconds) { html += ""; html += ""; html += ""; html += '"; } // Tokens (only show row if data exists) if (a.tokens || b.tokens) { html += ""; html += ""; html += ""; html += '"; } html += "
Metric" + escapeHtml(labelA) + "" + escapeHtml(labelB) + "Delta
Pass Rate" + fmtStat(a.pass_rate, true) + "" + fmtStat(b.pass_rate, true) + "' + (delta.pass_rate || "—") + "
Time (s)" + fmtStat(a.time_seconds, false) + "" + fmtStat(b.time_seconds, false) + "' + (delta.time_seconds ? delta.time_seconds + "s" : "—") + "
Tokens" + fmtStat(a.tokens, false) + "" + fmtStat(b.tokens, false) + "' + (delta.tokens || "—") + "
"; // Per-eval breakdown (if runs data available) const runs = data.runs || []; if (runs.length > 0) { const evalIds = [...new Set(runs.map(r => r.eval_id))].sort((a, b) => a - b); html += "

Per-Eval Breakdown

"; const hasTime = runs.some(r => r.result && r.result.time_seconds != null); const hasErrors = runs.some(r => r.result && r.result.errors > 0); for (const evalId of evalIds) { const evalRuns = runs.filter(r => r.eval_id === evalId); const evalName = evalRuns[0] && evalRuns[0].eval_name ? evalRuns[0].eval_name : "Eval " + evalId; html += "

" + escapeHtml(evalName) + "

"; html += ''; html += ""; if (hasTime) html += ""; if (hasErrors) html += ""; html += ""; html += ""; // Group by config and render with average rows const configGroups = [...new Set(evalRuns.map(r => r.configuration))]; for (let ci = 0; ci < configGroups.length; ci++) { const config = configGroups[ci]; const configRuns = evalRuns.filter(r => r.configuration === config); if (configRuns.length === 0) continue; const rowClass = ci === 0 ? "benchmark-row-with" : "benchmark-row-without"; const configLabel = config.replace(/_/g, " ").replace(/\b\w/g, c => c.toUpperCase()); for (const run of configRuns) { const r = run.result || {}; const prClass = r.pass_rate >= 0.8 ? "benchmark-delta-positive" : r.pass_rate < 0.5 ? "benchmark-delta-negative" : ""; html += ''; html += ""; html += ""; html += '"; if (hasTime) html += ""; if (hasErrors) html += ""; html += ""; } // Average row const rates = configRuns.map(r => (r.result || {}).pass_rate || 0); const avgRate = rates.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0) / rates.length; const avgPrClass = avgRate >= 0.8 ? "benchmark-delta-positive" : avgRate < 0.5 ? "benchmark-delta-negative" : ""; html += ''; html += ""; html += ""; html += '"; if (hasTime) { const times = configRuns.map(r => (r.result || {}).time_seconds).filter(t => t != null); html += ""; } if (hasErrors) html += ""; html += ""; } html += "
ConfigRunPass RateTime (s)Crashes During Execution

" + configLabel + "Avg' + (avgRate * 100).toFixed(0) + "%" + (times.length ? (times.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0) / times.length).toFixed(1) : "—") + "
"; // Per-assertion detail for this eval const runsWithExpectations = {}; for (const config of configGroups) { runsWithExpectations[config] = evalRuns.filter(r => r.configuration === config && r.expectations && r.expectations.length > 0); } const hasAnyExpectations = Object.values(runsWithExpectations).some(runs => runs.length > 0); if (hasAnyExpectations) { // Collect all unique assertion texts across all configs const allAssertions = []; const seen = new Set(); for (const config of configGroups) { for (const run of runsWithExpectations[config]) { for (const exp of (run.expectations || [])) { if (!seen.has(exp.text)) { seen.add(exp.text); allAssertions.push(exp.text); } } } } html += ''; html += ""; for (const config of configGroups) { const label = config.replace(/_/g, " ").replace(/\b\w/g, c => c.toUpperCase()); html += ""; } html += ""; for (const assertionText of allAssertions) { html += ""; for (const config of configGroups) { html += ""; } html += ""; } html += "
